# Change Log
**Purpose:** Append-only **decision log** — human decisions and approved canonical/structural changes only. It carries the *why* that a diff cannot. The record of *what* changed and *when* lives in `git log` (author, date, diff — and it cannot drift from itself).
**Format:** Date | File(s) changed | What changed | Trigger
**Narrowing rule (effective 2026-07-05):** From this date forward, log only decisions and approved canonical/structural changes here — not routine loop runs, ingests, or ordinary content edits (those live in `git log`). Entries dated before 2026-07-05 predate this rule and are preserved as-is; history is not rewritten.

---

| 2026-08-24 | `02-facilities/Suncor/Montreal-QC/B-102.md`; `tools/coil_geometry_audit.py` | **[Lane 4] B-102's per-circuit columns carried heater totals — and only three of the six "scale mismatches" I reported were real.** I told Jesse six tube-count rows were defective; on inspection **two were false positives of my own making** and one was a correctly-recorded card. `SCALE_QUALIFIER_RE` matched "total" anywhere in a cell, so it fired on B-1001's `18/circuit (36 heater-total ÷ 2 circuits)` — where 18 **is** per-circuit and matches Config Rollup — and on B-151, a single-pass heater where per-circuit and heater total are the same number by definition. Two wrong findings out of six is enough to discredit a report; the classifier now judges the qualifier **immediately following** the number. **H-28's `2 heater-wide` is correctly recorded and needs no change** — 2 tubes across 4 passes do not divide, and both card sections say so; heater-scale findings now split into `scale-mismatch` (our error, a per-circuit value exists) and `heater-scale only (irreducible)` (a customer-data gap), so nobody is sent to "fix" a right cell. **The genuine defect was one card, seven cells.** B-102's Tube Geometry held heater totals in `Tubes/Circuit` and `Length/Circuit` from the 2026-07-07 Suncor ingest (`7798d54`), contradicting its own Config Rollup: convection 16→8 tubes and 960→480 ft, radiant main run 32→16 and 2,934→1,467, outlet transition ~65→~32.5 ft, outlet header 12→6 and 694→347. `Avg Length` is a per-tube figure and was already correct. **One of the seven the audit never flagged** — convection `Length/Circuit` `960` stayed silent because that row's `Avg Length` satisfied the either-column test; fixed anyway, because the report is a floor and not a boundary. Totals and provenance moved to the keyed notes block with **stated vs derived marked**: `8`/pass, `6`/pass and `347` ft are stated, while `16`, `480`, `1,467` and `~32.5` are derived by halving a confirmed heater total across a symmetric 2-leg / 2-pass heater. **A standing cross-check now exists** — Tube Geometry's per-circuit tube counts summed by zone against Config Rollup's `Per circuit` row. Nothing did this before (`ROLLUP-SCALE` checks Config Rollup's internal arithmetic only), which is how B-102 survived seven weeks until a tool built for another purpose tripped over it. It caught B-102 (convection 16 vs 8, radiant 44 vs 22) and reports zero contradictions fleet-wide after the fix. Two defects were found in the cross-check itself while building it: it initially **skipped** B-102 by bailing on non-clean cells — the defect case — and it **invented** three contradictions by prefix-matching combined `Convection + Radiant` rollup rows to one zone, charging a whole circuit's count to convection (HP-0003 reconciles exactly). | Jesse, 2026-08-24: act on the six scale-mismatch rows. |

| 2026-08-23 | `~/.claude/skills/usadebusk-estimating/SKILL.md`; `01-context/estimating-approach.md`; `02-facilities/Valero/Three-Rivers-TX/*` (new `DSP26100.md` + 3 heater cards + facility); `01-context/active-jobs.md`; `00-inbox/2026-07-24-parallel-friction-factor-deferred.md` | **[gated] Four Lane 4 duration-model rulings, surfaced by walking a live estimate against Jesse's own finished quote.** Applying the skill as written produced **168 project hours against Jesse's 94** on DSP#26100 (Valero Three Rivers, sent 2026-08-22, $415,220.82). **(1) The 25–40% parallel-friction allowance is STRUCK.** Jesse: *"I don't remember ever making a rule like that."* A **designated pigging tech is assigned to each pass**, runs their own pump and circuit and changes their own pig in 30 s–5 min; double mode is two people doing one job each and **takes no longer than single mode**. Origin matters: per this log's own 2026-07-28 entry the band came out of a **regression-replay session to suppress model output variance** (open judgment returned 15/25/40% across three runs of two fixtures), with the crew-attention mechanism back-filled as justification — and `00-inbox/2026-07-24-parallel-friction-factor-deferred.md` had already recorded, four days earlier, that the supporting rollup rows were service- and size-confounded and that *"Claude reached for exactly +25% twice, unprompted... a model artifact, not evidence."* The band was written over the top of that note. Its evidence does not survive: the cited multi-pass rows (47 at mode 3, 52 at mode 5) are the 12,036 ft and 14,280 ft heaters while a **mode-1 row sits at 51**, and the "mode-1 average near 132" rests almost entirely on HP-0025's 259. **(2) The rate derate is GATED — apply it only when you have reason to believe the coil is dirty** (prior actuals, known history, hard service seen on that unit). A first-time unknown heater takes the round **100 ft/hr**; under T&M an overrun goes back to the customer and gets approved, so under-estimating is recoverable and over-estimating costs the bid. **`first-time clean with no prior data` is removed from the trigger list entirely** — it is the condition under which the derate does *not* apply, and that inversion caused most of the 79% overrun. Confirmed by the workup's own raw quotients at `U38`/`U39`: 2,561÷100 and 1,775÷100, no derate. **(3) Coil rounding is to the NEAREST EVEN hour, up or down** (replaces always-round-up), so totals land cleanly on 12-hr increments; practice carries judgment on top (17.75→18 is nearest-even, 25.61→24 is not, both Jesse's). **(4) The whole-shift landing and its mid-band diagnostic are REMOVED** — *"there is a certain time to do it, and not to do it and I won't know until I'm presented with the specific circumstance"*; DSP#26100 runs 26/38/30 for a project of 94. Shifts and days in the Total Duration block are a **presentation rounding only**. Two rules ADDED from Jesse's reasoning: **pre-inspection pigging on an already-decoked coil is a task allowance, not footage ÷ rate** (steam-air decokes leave *layered fouling*, the coil must be clean for the smart pig to read, the rep always approves more, and T&M means unworked hours are not billed — so the exposure points DOWN, not up); and **the first heater on a mobilization takes the full rig-in tier while subsequent heaters in close proximity take a reduced figure** (6 vs 4 on DSP#26100), which is why `Rig-Over` reads 0 on a job where the equipment moves twice. Regression fixtures `f1` and `f6` both encode the struck rules and are invalidated — re-cut from fresh replays, not hand-patched. **DSP#26100 is now the calibration reference.** Verified 2026-08-23: the amended path reproduces **H-1102 exactly** (1,775÷100=17.75→18, total 30) and **100-H-2 within 2 hrs** (25.61→26 against Jesse's shaved 24, total 40 v 38). 100-H-1 is not a test — its 8-hr allowance is now written into the skill, so a replay returns it by construction. Against a pre-correction 168 project hours, the amended skill lands 96 against the quote's 94. | Jesse asked to be walked through the duration logic line by line, rejected three mechanics, then supplied his finished workup and quotation as ground truth. |
| 2026-08-21 | `04-knowledge/_canonical-heater-card.md`; `templates/_heater-template.md`; `~/.claude/skills/usadebusk-vault-ingest/SKILL.md`; 20 cards under `02-facilities/`; `tools/vault_lint.py` + 2 fixtures; `50-dashboards/heater-fleet.base` | **[Lane 4] Heater-card frontmatter conformance — `heater-type` normalized 11 values to 5, `verified` typed, both lint-locked, and the fleet gets a Bases view.** Trying Obsidian Bases surfaced two defects that are invisible one card at a time and obvious once 41 cards sit in a column. **Neither was purely a data problem — the spec was wrong on both counts.** (1) `heater-type` was already specced `crude | vacuum | reboiler | other`, but **`coker` was missing from the vocabulary** while `usadebusk-estimating` names coker as a fouling derate class ("harder fouling (coker / crude / vacuum)"), so five cards used it off-schema. `coker` added; 10 cards migrated. (2) **`verified` was absent from the exemplar's frontmatter block entirely** while `OP-FRONTMATTER` already lint-checked its *presence* — a required field nobody had specified, which is precisely why ten cards filled it in reasonably and differently, one with forty words about a 1976 BOM cross-check. Now strictly `YYYY-MM-DD` or `never`; the prose moved verbatim to each card's `## Notes` as a `**Verification:**` paragraph. **A mapping I recommended was overturned by recon before it was applied, and that is the substance of this entry.** I proposed "charge heater → crude" off the string alone. Reading the `service` field showed four of those six cards read **Unifiner / Platformer / NHT** — naphtha and hydrotreating service, not crude — so folding them into `crude` would have put four non-crude heaters into the crude fouling derate, a pricing-relevant error introduced by a cleanup. Only `HF-009A/B` ("Crude Charge") are genuinely crude; the other four went to `other` alongside `F-501` (Hydrofining) and `H-2421` (HDU/GOH Charge, whose value `vertical-cylindrical` was firebox geometry in a service field). Jesse ruled `other` over a new `hydrotreater` class knowing it grows `other` to 15 of 41. **Locked with two new error-tier lint rules** — `HEATER-TYPE-VOCAB` and `VERIFIED-FORMAT`, each with a fixture, on DQ-006's `TUBE-GEOM-HEADER` precedent: both fire on zero files after migration, so they are pure drift protection with no backlog, and an error that starts at zero can only ever mean someone typed a new value. Value rules skip `_`-prefixed files, since the exemplar and template hold placeholder syntax that by construction cannot satisfy them. **Built last, deliberately: `50-dashboards/heater-fleet.base`** with four views (By client · By type · Never verified · Verification log). It contains **no formulas** — built before the migration it would have needed nested `if()` chains to collapse `vacuum heater` into `vacuum` and parse a date out of a sentence, all to be deleted afterwards. **Not fixed, and not a defect:** 24 of 41 cards still read `verified: never`. That is real work, not bad data, and the base's Never-verified view is now the working list. **Also found, not fixed:** the schema states `tags: [heater-card, <Client>, <heater-type>]` but only 1 of 41 cards actually carries the heater-type in its tags — a third conformance gap, out of scope here. **Verified:** lint 0 errors, self-test 18/18 rules firing, all 41 `heater-type` in vocabulary (other 15 · crude 9 · vacuum 6 · reboiler 6 · coker 5), all 41 `verified` matching the format, every displaced string present verbatim in Notes, and `estimating-actuals-rollup.md` byte-identical — nothing in it reads either field, so any movement would have meant collateral damage. | Jesse asked how to try Obsidian Bases; building a preview of the view surfaced both defects. |
| 2026-08-21 | `tools/estimating_rollup.py`; `04-knowledge/_canonical-heater-card.md`; `02-facilities/HF-Sinclair/Artesia-NM/{H19.md, H20.md}`; `00-inbox/idea-rig-out-vs-rig-in-mirror-rule.md`; `50-dashboards/decision-queue.md` | **DQ-017 Phase 3 — the actuals rollup now says what KIND of number each rig figure is, and the mirror-rule question closes with no skill change.** The Rig-In column has been known mixed-method since 2026-08-16; cells that print alike were produced five different ways, and the rollup rendered them as one series. Ruled **mark, don't suppress** (Jesse): a `Rig method` column, every figure still visible. **Four of six values derive from markers the cards already carried** — `combined` from `combined-heaters`, `2-rig-sum` from `hours-blended` + `Rigs > 1`, `blended` from single-rig `hours-blended`, `see card` from a `*` on a rig cell. Only the HF Sinclair quarantine needed a new token, reusing the existing comma-append mechanism (`, rig-quarantined`) rather than inventing one. **Building it corrected the plan twice, and both corrections are the substance.** (a) The plan read `*` as "bundled rigging figure." Wrong — `*` means *the card footnotes this cell*, and the reasons differ: Valero bundles rig-in + rig-over + rig-out into one "Rigging" number, while the Flint Hills pair (01-BA-105 / 02-BA-201) is **one continuous rig-over where each heater's missing side is absent by design and correct per schema**. One label would have invented a defect where the convention was working; resolved to `see card` rather than guess. (b) The plan defaulted the column to `clean`. **HF-0011 killed that** — its Rig-Out reads `3`, the *billed* figure, with a further **8 hrs customer-signed and never billed**, and nothing on that row says so. Default is now `unmarked` = *no method problem recorded*, explicitly not *verified*; a `clean` default would have swept that row into a rule. **The derivable population is 5 pairs across 3 jobs, and the script computes it rather than asserting it** — 6 rows are `unmarked` but HP-0007 records no Rig-In, so `unmarked` ≠ derivable. **Mirror-rule gate discharged with a no-change finding.** `usadebusk-estimating` already held the correct position: SKILL.md:68 names F-802 as the counter-example and concludes one row is not enough to move the rule while rig-out remains the exposure to state. Inspection was rejected as the method because Claude built most of the system and would be grading its own work. **Headline: the problem is rate, not worth.** The 2026-08-15 sweep cleared the review pile to zero and the queue to two; five days later both were at ten, because capture, idea-research and pre-staging have each fired daily since 08-10 while the only clearing resource is Jesse. Jesse's ruling: **the gate is real and correctly placed, but it gates dewatering, not rig-out.** Acceptance is given against the inspection vendor's preliminary digital report, which confirms both that the vendor captured usable data and that no fouling remains; a non-acceptance does not release anything — pigging continues and the tool runs again until the customer has the data. The customer's pipe-fitters offload them from the USADebusk support trailer, hang them at rig-in (usually concurrently with USADebusk hose routing, not ahead of it), remove them at rig-out, help place them back on the support unit, and reconnect the heater piping. The reason is physical and is now recorded with the rule: **the units weigh over 400 lbs and the fitters have crane and forklift access USADebusk does not.** USADebusk's connection boundary is the **3" Fig. 200 connection on the launcher/receiver** — we make and break the hose, they make and break the flange. §7.1's single conflated row split into four; §7.3 gained a Pipe-fitting subsection; §7.4 no longer implies USADebusk installs launchers and explicitly declines to say whether the fitters and the lifting contractor are the same party, which varies by facility. Who the fitters are is the customer's call, usually unknown until arrival — the facility's own crew or the turnaround pipefitting contractor — and USADebusk has no contact with them and no say, so they are called *the customer* in customer-facing documents. **`usadebusk-estimating` had this right the whole time** (its pipefitter adder prices exactly this wait, and line 85 already warned against shaving it off the rig-out side), so **no bid was mispriced** — the drift was confined to the procedural layer. | 2026-08-15 | `~/.claude/skills/usadebusk-estimating/SKILL.md`; 06-insights/2026-08-08-prestaged-f6-rig-tier-decision.md | **The rig-in tier rule was missing its dominant driver, and that — not fixture wording — was the F6 12-vs-10 divergence.** The rule set the tier from two drivers (connection elevation, run distance from the pumper); Jesse named a third: **mode**, i.e. pumps utilized, which sets circuit-line and spool count and therefore how much hose gets built. A circuit line runs from a pumper port to one launcher or receiver connection: single = 1 pump / 2 lines / 2 spools, double = 2 / 4 / 4, triple = 3 / 6 / 6. Hoses are 17 ft, hard pipe 20 ft, a 60 ft run is ~4–5 hoses **per line**. Critically the three drivers **multiply rather than offset** — elevation adds to run length (60 ft out + 60 ft up = 120 ft, doubling hose per line) and mode multiplies by line count — so the long-standing "one driver low, one high, call it the middle" reading is an error, and it is what produced four inconsistent F6 replays. Also recorded: **rig-in never exceeds 12 hrs and rarely exceeds 8**, so Very large is a ceiling rather than a rung reached by accumulation, and the pipefitter adder stacks *inside* the cap (Very large + fitter wait = 12, not 14). F6 under the corrected rule: rig-in 12, rig-out 12, raw 45, landing 48 on a +3 adjustment that no longer trips the mid-band diagnostic. Jesse's first-stated triple-mode hose count (12–15) contradicted his own primitives (6 lines × 4–5 = 24–30); flagged in session, **confirmed a slip, corrected to 24–30**, and the per-mode figures at a 60 ft run (single 8–10 / double 16–20 / triple 24–30) are now in the skill. | Pre-staged review note 2026-08-08 (F6 rig-tier ambiguity) walked in session; Jesse supplied the mode/hose mechanics and the 12-hr cap, which reframed the question from a fixture amendment to a rule gap. |

| 2026-07-24 | ~/.claude skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md; ~/.claude skills/usadebusk-estimating/SKILL.md; ~/.claude regression/adversarial-review-arm-test-2026-07-24.md (new); 06-insights/2026-07-24-idea-research-lint-lock-heater-schema.md; 00-inbox/idea-orphaned-equipment-rules-proposal-path.md (new) | **[gated] `adversarial-review` validated for the first time and demoted; one cross-cutting estimating gap closed.** The skill was adopted unvalidated and had never been tested against the alternative it implicitly beats. Three arms run blind on two regression deliverables with recorded defect lists: the incumbent Finder/Adversary/Referee chain found **3 of 6** recorded defects at **3.3x** the tokens and 3x the wall clock; a single agent with a grounding requirement found **5 of 6**; a plain single agent **5.5 of 6**. Two architectural causes, both measured not inferred: the chain is **subtractive** (Adversary and Referee only filter, so recall is capped by the Finder and two further Opus calls are spent lowering it), and **bundling destroys true positives** (a finding packaging a true claim with a false one gets disproved whole). Jesse's call: demote, don't delete — single grounded verifier is now the default arm, the chain is the opt-in precision pass, and a claim-level adjudication rule was added. Follow-up run settled the one variable the test had held constant: swapping the Adversary to **Fable 5** took the chain from 1/4 to 3/4 on F1 — the Finder's ceiling — at identical cost, so cross-model is now the default whenever the chain runs, and the skill's prior claim that same-model "gets most of the benefit on its own" is retired as measurably false. A **Phase 4.4 empirical gate** was added: agreement among same-family agents is one reading, not three. `Refute-or-Promote` (arXiv 2604.19049, Agarwal) verified real and its abstract read — its ten-reviewer unanimous false positive killed by one empirical test is the cited basis for that gate; results tables still unopened and the record says so. **Separately and higher-value commercially:** all three arms flagged that the F1 proposal never asked for a 2" fire hose, and the cause was structural — the rule lived only in `usadebusk-equipment`, which a proposal build (core + estimating) never loads, so Jesse's 2026-07-23 ruling was unreachable from the workflow needing it and every firewater bid would omit the ask. Section 8 now carries the trigger. Wider orphaned-rule audit deferred to `00-inbox/`. Lint-lock review closed as already-built (shipped `b7f5df3` 2026-07-20). | 2026-07-19 | mcp scheduled-tasks (vault-skill-drift-loop, disabled); tools/vault_health.py; 04-knowledge/vault-skill-drift-loop-spec.md; 01-context/system-workflow-reference.md | **[gated] Decision: the Skill-Drift Loop becomes on-demand; its monthly schedule is disabled.** Jesse asked whether the nightly/monthly loops would stall on approval prompts when running unattended. Audit of all four scheduled tasks against the live permission rules: capture, idea-research, and consolidation are covered by the vault's project + local settings (folder-scoped Write/Edit, the `py -3 tools/*.py` commands, and vault-scoped git add/commit/push). Skill-drift is not, and cannot cleanly be: it is the only loop that writes **outside** the vault — `drift/YYYY-MM` branch creation, `skills/**` edits, and commit/push in the config repo — which collides head-on with the same-day decision scoping git mutation authority to the vault. Two structural findings drove the call: (1) permission patterns match literal command-string prefixes, so the spec's `git -C ~/.claude …` form is matched by **neither** the global allow rules nor the global deny rules (force-push/amend/reset/branch-delete), meaning any `-C`-form allow would need its denies rewritten in the same form or the guardrail silently would not apply; (2) permission scope is path-based and cannot be branch-scoped, so granting the loop `Edit(skills/**)` would equally let any interactive session edit Lane 4 domain truth (pricing, safety, SOP values) on `main` with no prompt. Rejected alternatives: a narrow carve-out in global settings (erodes both guardrails), and a branch-aware PreToolUse hook (precise, but a second piece of hook code to maintain and debug at 3am). Chose the zero-new-permission path — run it manually, one deliberate invocation a month. Follow-through: removed from `LOOP_HEARTBEATS` in vault_health.py so a disabled scheduler no longer reports a false `FAIL: scheduler silent`; spec Trigger rewritten with the reasoning; workflow reference now reads three scheduled + two on-demand. There is no per-scheduled-task permission mode — `permissions.defaultMode` is session-wide, so loosening it would have loosened interactive sessions too. Revert path: `git revert` this commit and re-enable the task via `mcp__scheduled-tasks`. | Session: scheduled-loop permission audit |

Untangling needs a crash job at a large bore or a routine job at a small one; that is the real gate on any rate model and it is a data gate, not a method gate. The only consumer of a deferred operational item was a loop that fires when Jesse says a phrase out loud, so the analysis machinery sat idle while the input pile grew. **The design turns on one distinction: deciding is Lane 4 and stays with Jesse; preparing the decision is not.** The loop reads inbox items carrying `<!-- vault-loop: -->`, drafts one review note in `06-insights/` with real cited Source Material, appends one `decision-queue.md` row, and marks the item `<!-- vault-prestaged: -->` so it is never reprocessed. Separate task carries its own heartbeat, cadence and kill switch. **Two hard bounds, neither of them new machinery:** the existing 10-row queue cap is the throttle (at cap the loop no-ops before even reading the inbox, so a runaway fills ten slots and halts), and one item per run oldest-first matches the Idea Research Loop's proven shape — at the current pile of 16 the backlog clears in about two weeks unattended. **The skip disposition is the anti-ceremony guard** raised in the proposal's own Risks section: an item that is an execution correction (`2026-07-24-dsp26085-submitted-wrong-quote-number` is the worked example — a fix to make, not a decision to weigh) or already covered gets marked and reported as a to-do, with no review note and no queue row, because sixteen mediocre review notes are worse than sixteen raw inbox notes when each carries an implied claim of analysis. **Pre-staged notes are marked as unreviewed inference** — `review_type: pre-staged`, `source_authority: inferred`, `status: open` are mandatory frontmatter, so a future session cannot mistake a machine-drafted Source Material table for settled vault truth. **Scheduled 06:00 daily, deliberately one hour behind capture's 05:00**, since capture is what applies the defer markers this loop reads; both sit before working hours after the 08:00 collision earlier the same day. All eight would be marked "read it" and pass. They carry the longest survival in the data — 5 and 6 turns against 1–2 for the `RECON: no` cases — and the highest cost, one standing while it was committed to git twice and one shipping under the heading "Verified rather than assumed." **Mechanism:** the source supports something narrower than the claim built on it. A claim asserted without reading dies the moment anyone opens the file; a claim built on an *agreeing* source cannot be falsified by re-reading that source, so refutation requires a different category of artifact (the Quest isometric, the signed change order) and the failure is not knowing one is needed. **Back-tested per [[feedback-backtest-before-build]].** The first draft keyed on "a claim resting on a computed figure" and caught **3 of 8** — missing the referent error (#16, right document, wrong facility), the scope over-read (#31, an archive note covering only the quote-number-defective version), and the two cases with no figure in them. Rejected below the pre-set 5-of-8 bar rather than adopted. The committed wording keys on the overreach instead of the figure type and catches **6 of 8**, missing #21 (argument from my own session state) and #28 (Jesse's intent asserted from documents that cannot carry intent). **Recorded limit:** this corpus contains only wrong claims, so it measures recall and cannot measure precision — there is no negative set, unlike the WORD-DELTA back-test that replayed 43 commits including correct ones. The verification step as originally written asked how many of the other 28 it fires on, treating them as false positives; they are errors too, so that number measures nothing. Accepted anyway because this is prose guidance, not a firing gate — DQ-007's objection to a 100%-firing rule applies to alarms that interrupt, not to phrasing. Revert path: `git revert f43db6c` in the config repo. | Continuation of the same session's instruction audit. After the first clause was committed, the eight `RECON: yes` cases were analysed as the one class Jesse had twice been told was worth real time and that had not been done. |

For all 3 sides / pumps of the Trimax units, each side (left, center, right) has a Blue and Red port."* The knowledge layer had this **inverted**: it named the ports after sections and then treated the binding as fixed plumbing, so `equipment-library` could say both *"one CONV, one RAD"* and *"both serve as either feed or return"* three lines apart without the contradiction registering. **The second error is the one that mattered.** With launcher bound to convection and receiver to radiant, every rig-in sequence in the vault assumed one spool at each end of the heater — and **on a looped circuit both land at the same end while the 180s take the other, so the looped end carries no spool at all.** That consequence was stated nowhere. **There is no default loop end, and the facility layer proves it**: Valero Three Rivers (`_facility.md:105`) and ExxonMobil F-501 (`F-501.md:87`) loop at the *convection inlets*; CAD26001, F-901 and F-802 loop at the *radiant outlets*. Roughly 50/50 across real jobs, while `industry-foundation.md` and `manual/17-glossary.md` both defined a jumper spool as one connecting *"two radiant outlet flanges"* — a coin-flip written as a definition. **A live field-facing error was fixed in the same pass:** the CAD26001 job sheet's Fig 1 caption, in the markdown *and* the printable HTML a crew reads, said *"receivers and loop 180s at the radiant outlet flanges"*, which cannot both be true and contradicted line 95 of its own file. **§8.4 Direction of travel had no looped case at all** — on a loop the pig is recovered at the same kind of flange it launched from, which §8.4 called the launching end. **Skills corrected in the same breath, deliberately:** `rig-diagram-corpus.md:115` names `usadebusk-sop` as the source the vault files derive from, so leaving the skills disagreeing would have re-seeded both errors on the next ingest. D4 is physically impossible (a circuit needs one feed and one return) and was a *consequence* of D3's premise; fixing D3 fixed D4. **Port count flagged rather than asserted:** the card read "two ports at rear of trailer", which counts one assembly's pair as the whole trailer; six follows from Jesse's statement but has not been eyeballed on a unit, and is marked as such. **Verified:** no residual port-as-section or fixed-placement claims outside the corrections themselves; both counter-example facility notes re-read to confirm nothing written contradicts the evidence it rests on; lint 0 errors; 10 SharePoint projections regenerated.
